# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {
    count : int
    stack : List<FillableCell>
    getUndoCount() : int
    pop() : FillableCell
    push(cell : FillableCell) : void
}
class Coordinate {
    col : int
    row : int
    add(other : Coordinate) : Coordinate
    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean
}
class DelayBar {
    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}
class FillableCell {
    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Game {
    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue
    hasLost() : boolean
    hasWon() : boolean
    placePipe(row : int, col : char) : boolean
    skipPipe() : void
    undoStep() : boolean
    updateState() : void
}
class Map {
    cell : cellarray
    cols : int
    prevFilledDistance : EIntegerObject
    prevFilledTiles : int
    rows : int
    filledTiles : List<Coordinate>
    sinkCell : TerminationCell
    sourceCell : TerminationCell
    checkPath() : boolean
    fillBeginTile() : void
    fillTiles(distance : int) : void
    hasLost() : boolean
    tryPlacePipe(row : int, col : int, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    tryPlacePipe1(coord : Coordinate, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    undo(coord : Coordinate) : void
}
abstract class MapElement {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Pa19Helper {
    cellFromChar(c : char, coord : Coordinate, terminationType : TerminationType) : Cell
    createFillableCell(coord : Coordinate, pipes : Pipe) : FillableCell
    createGame(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cells : cellarray, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    createMap(rows : int, cols : int, cells : cellarray) : Map
    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell
    createWall(coord : Coordinate) : Wall
    gameFromString(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cellsRep : String, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}
class Pipe {
    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class PipeQueue {
    maxGenLength : int
    pipeQueue : List<Pipe>
    consume() : void
    generateNewPipe() : Pipe
    peek() : Pipe
    undo(pipe : Pipe) : void
}
class TerminationCell {
    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction
    type : TerminationType
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Wall {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
enum Direction {
    UP
    DOWN
    LEFT
    RIGHT
}
enum PipeShape {
    HORIZONTAL
    VERTICAL
    TOP_LEFT
    TOP_RIGHT
    BOTTOM_LEFT
    BOTTOM_RIGHT
    CROSS
}
enum TerminationType {
    SOURCE
    SINK
}

Cell --|> MapElement
Cell *-- Coordinate : coord
CellStack *-- "*" FillableCell : stack
FillableCell --|> Cell
FillableCell *-- Pipe : pipe
Game *-- CellStack : cellStack
Game *-- DelayBar : delayBar
Game *-- Map : map
Game *-- PipeQueue : pipeQueue
Map *-- "*" Coordinate : filledTiles
Map --> TerminationCell : sinkCell
Map --> TerminationCell : sourceCell
Pipe --|> MapElement
PipeQueue *-- "*" Pipe : pipeQueue
TerminationCell --|> Cell
Wall --|> Cell
@enduml
```

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

```text

 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
Map::fillTiles
Summary: Propagates water from the SOURCE outward up to a specified distance using BFS with direction-matching logic.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: sourceCell, filledTiles, prevFilledTiles
Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell, Pa19Helper
Input: distance : int - maximum BFS radius for water propagation.
Output: void
Pre-condition: `distance > 0`; `sourceCell` is initialized and accessible.
Post-condition: All reachable pipes within `distance` marked filled; `filledTiles` and counters updated.

Map::tryPlacePipe
Summary: Validates coordinates, cell type, and occupancy before committing a pipe placement.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: cells, rows, cols
Related Classes: FillableCell, Pipe, Coordinate
Input: row : int, col : int (playable indices), pipe : Pipe
Output: boolean - true if placement accepted, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: `pipe` is not null; coordinates align with playable grid area.
Post-condition: Pipe reference attached to target cell on success; no change on failure.

Map::hasLost
Summary: Evaluates loss condition based on stagnant water flow in the previous round.
Algorithm: Return `prevFilledTiles == 0`. (Loss is only evaluated externally when `delayBar.distance() > 0`).
Related Features: prevFilledTiles
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if no new tiles were filled previously, indicating loss.
Pre-condition: At least one full update cycle has completed.
Post-condition: No state change.

Map::undo
Summary: Clears a previously placed pipe from a specific coordinate during an undo operation.
Algorithm: `cells[coord.row][coord.col].pipe = null`
Related Features: cells
Related Classes: Coordinate, FillableCell
Input: coord : Coordinate - position of the cell to revert.
Output: void
Pre-condition: `coord` points to a valid FillableCell on the map.
Post-condition: Pipe reference at the specified coordinate is set to null.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
```

Algorithm: 
1. Initialize BFS queue with `sourceCell.coord` (assume already marked filled by `fillBeginTile()`). Initialize `newlyFilled` list.
2. Set `currentDistance = 0`.
3. While `currentDistance < distance` and queue is not empty:
   a. Process all coordinates currently in the queue (BFS layer processing).
   b. Dequeue `currCoord`. Get `currCell = cells[currCoord.row][currCoord.col]`.
   c. For each `dir` in `currCell.pipe.getConnections()`:
      i. `nextCoord = currCoord.add(Pa19Helper.getOffset(dir))`.
      ii. Validate `nextCoord` bounds and ensure `cells[nextCoord.row][nextCoord.col]` is a `FillableCell`.
      iii. If valid, get `nextPipe`. Check if `Pa19Helper.getOpposite(dir)` exists in `nextPipe.getConnections()`.
      iv. If matching and `nextCell` not yet filled, mark filled, add to `newlyFilled`, enqueue `nextCoord`.
   d. Increment `currentDistance`.
4. Append all entries from `newlyFilled` to `filledTiles`.
5. Update `prevFilledTiles` to `newlyFilled.size()`.
Related Features: sourceCell, filledTiles, prevFilledTiles, cells
Related Classes: Coordinate, Direction, Pipe, TerminationCell, Pa19Helper
Input: distance : int - maximum BFS radius/distance for water propagation.
Output: void
Pre-condition: `distance >= 0`; `sourceCell` is initialized and accessible.
Post-condition: All reachable pipes within `distance` marked filled; `filledTiles` updated with new coordinates; `prevFilledTiles` updated to reflect tiles filled in this propagation step., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@b4732dc (name: undo) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Clears a previously placed pipe from a specific coordinate during an undo operation.
Algorithm: 
1. Access `targetCell = cells[coord.row][coord.col]`.
2. Set `targetCell.pipe = null`.
Related Features: cells
Related Classes: Coordinate, FillableCell
Input: coord : Coordinate - position of the cell to revert.
Output: void
Pre-condition: `coord` points to a valid FillableCell on the map.
Post-condition: Pipe reference at the specified coordinate is set to null; no other state changes.}

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
CellStack::push
Summary: Records a placed pipe cell onto the undo history stack.
Algorithm: Append `cell` to the end of the `stack` list.
Related Features: stack
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: cell : FillableCell - The cell object representing a successfully placed pipe on the map.
Output: void
Pre-condition: `cell` is not null and corresponds to a valid placement action.
Post-condition: `stack` contains `cell` at its tail.

CellStack::pop
Summary: Removes the most recently placed FillableCell from the stack and prepares it for undo processing.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: stack, count
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: None
Output: FillableCell representing the popped cell, or null/exception if empty (caller should guard).
Pre-condition: `stack` contains at least one element.
Post-condition: The topmost cell is removed from `stack`, and `count` is incremented by 1.

CellStack::getUndoCount
Summary: Returns the total number of successful undo operations performed since the game started.
Algorithm: Return the value of the `count` attribute directly.
Related Features: count
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: int representing the cumulative count of successful undo actions.
Pre-condition: The CellStack instance is properly initialized.
Post-condition: No modification to the object's state or attributes.

# Operation Specifications
CellStack::push
Summary: Records a placed pipe cell onto the undo history stack.
Algorithm: Append `cell` to the end of the `stack` list.
Related Features: stack
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: cell : FillableCell - The cell object representing a successfully placed pipe on the map.
Output: void
Pre-condition: `cell` is not null and corresponds to a valid placement action.
Post-condition: `stack` contains `cell` at its tail.

CellStack::pop
Summary: Removes the most recently placed FillableCell from the stack and prepares it for undo processing.
Algorithm: 
Related Features: stack, count
Related Classes: FillableCell
Input: None
Output: FillableCell representing the popped cell, or null/exception if empty (caller should guard).
Pre-condition: `stack` contains at least one element.
Post-condition: The topmost cell is removed from `stack`, and `count` is incremented by 1.

CellStack::getUndoCount
Summary: Returns the total number of successful undo operations performed since the game started.
Algorithm: Return the value of the `count` attribute directly.
Related Features: count
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: int representing the cumulative count of successful undo actions.
Pre-condition: The CellStack instance is properly initialized.
Post-condition: No modification to the object's state or attributes.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title pa19 Model

abstract class Cell {
    coord : Coordinate
}
class CellStack {
    count : int
    stack : List<FillableCell>
    getUndoCount() : int
    pop() : FillableCell
    push(cell : FillableCell) : void
}
class Coordinate {
    col : int
    row : int
    add(other : Coordinate) : Coordinate
    equals(obj : EJavaObject) : boolean
}
class DelayBar {
    currentValue : int
    initialValue : int
    countdown() : void
    distance() : int
}
class FillableCell {
    pipe : Pipe
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Game {
    numOfSteps : int
    cellStack : CellStack
    delayBar : DelayBar
    map : Map
    pipeQueue : PipeQueue
    hasLost() : boolean
    hasWon() : boolean
    placePipe(row : int, col : char) : boolean
    skipPipe() : void
    undoStep() : boolean
    updateState() : void
}
class Map {
    cell : cellarray
    cols : int
    prevFilledDistance : EIntegerObject
    prevFilledTiles : int
    rows : int
    filledTiles : List<Coordinate>
    sinkCell : TerminationCell
    sourceCell : TerminationCell
    checkPath() : boolean
    fillBeginTile() : void
    fillTiles(distance : int) : void
    hasLost() : boolean
    tryPlacePipe(row : int, col : int, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    tryPlacePipe1(coord : Coordinate, pipe : Pipe) : boolean
    undo(coord : Coordinate) : void
}
abstract class MapElement {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Pa19Helper {
    cellFromChar(c : char, coord : Coordinate, terminationType : TerminationType) : Cell
    createFillableCell(coord : Coordinate, pipes : Pipe) : FillableCell
    createGame(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cells : cellarray, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    createMap(rows : int, cols : int, cells : cellarray) : Map
    createTerminationCell(coord : Coordinate, type : TerminationType, dir : Direction) : TerminationCell
    createWall(coord : Coordinate) : Wall
    gameFromString(rows : int, cols : int, delay : int, cellsRep : String, pipes : List<Pipe>) : Game
    getOffset(dir : Direction) : Coordinate
    getOpposite(dir : Direction) : Direction
    mapFromString(rows : int, cols : int, cellsRep : String) : Map
    pipeFromString(rep : String) : Pipe
}
class Pipe {
    filled : boolean
    shape : PipeShape
    getConnections() : List<Direction>
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class PipeQueue {
    maxGenLength : int
    pipeQueue : List<Pipe>
    consume() : void
    generateNewPipe() : Pipe
    peek() : Pipe
    undo(pipe : Pipe) : void
}
class TerminationCell {
    filled : boolean
    pointingTo : Direction
    type : TerminationType
    toSingleChar() : char
}
class Wall {
    toSingleChar() : char
}
enum Direction {
    UP
    DOWN
    LEFT
    RIGHT
}
enum PipeShape {
    HORIZONTAL
    VERTICAL
    TOP_LEFT
    TOP_RIGHT
    BOTTOM_LEFT
    BOTTOM_RIGHT
    CROSS
}
enum TerminationType {
    SOURCE
    SINK
}

Cell --|> MapElement
Cell *-- Coordinate : coord
CellStack *-- "*" FillableCell : stack
FillableCell --|> Cell
FillableCell *-- Pipe : pipe
Game *-- CellStack : cellStack
Game *-- DelayBar : delayBar
Game *-- Map : map
Game *-- PipeQueue : pipeQueue
Map *-- "*" Coordinate : filledTiles
Map --> TerminationCell : sinkCell
Map --> TerminationCell : sourceCell
Pipe --|> MapElement
PipeQueue *-- "*" Pipe : pipeQueue
TerminationCell --|> Cell
Wall --|> Cell
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
FillableCell::toSingleChar
Summary: Renders the visual character representation of the cell's current pipe state.
Algorithm: Delegate rendering to `pipe.toSingleChar()`.
Related Features: pipe
Related Classes: Pipe
Input: None
Output: char representing the pipe shape and filled/unfilled status.
Pre-condition: `pipe` attribute is initialized and not null.
Post-condition: No state change.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
 Overall/Domain description.
 Console pipe-connection game: grid with outer walls, one source inside pointing inward, one sink on the border
  pointing outward. Player places pipes from a queue; after a delay, water flows along placed pipes.
Cell[][] uses 0-based array indices, but index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are occupied by the outer wall border. Therefore, the playable area starts at internal index 1: [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]. Player-facing coordinates are deliberately aligned with these playable indices: row 1 maps to internal row 1, column 'A' maps to internal col 1. No row-1/col-1 conversion is performed.
 Direction provides four directions plus opposite/offset; PipeShape defines
  connection
  directions; TerminationType distinguishes SOURCE vs SINK.

  Functional Requirements

Coordinate and Direction: Coordinate.row and Coordinate.col represent internal Cell[][] array indices and are used directly as cells[row][col]. A Cell stored at cells[r][c] should have Coordinate(row=r, col=c). 
Because the outer wall border occupies index 0, playable cells naturally start at index 1, so player input aligns with internal playable indices.
  Direction enum provides four directions (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT) and Pa19Helper provides getOpposite() returning the opposite direction
  and getOffset() returning the unit coordinate offset for movement calculations.

  Game: Operations to place a pipe, skip, undo, advance water flow, check win, check loss; convenience fromString
  constructor; maintain step count. 
Operation placePipe: This method maps the player column letter to an internal column index using col - 'A' + 1, 
keeps row unchanged, and places the pipe at cells[row][colIndex]. For example, placePipe(1, 'A') targets cells[1][1].
Operation skipPipe: Consumes the current pipe from the queue and increments the step count. Operation
  undoStep: Pops the last placed cell from CellStack, restores the pipe to the queue head, clears the cell on the map,
  and increments the step count. Returns false if there are no steps to undo.

  Map: Cells are stored as a 2D array Cell[][] with indices [0..rows-1] × [0..cols-1],
  where index 0 and rows-1/cols-1 are walls (border). The playable area occupies
  internal indices [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], which aligns with 1-based
  user input mapping. tryPlacePipe(row, col, pipe) accepts playable-area aligned coordinates (row and col in [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2]) and uses them
  directly as array indices (no conversion needed due to the wall alignment).
  It rejects: (1) row/col outside [1..rows-2] × [1..cols-2], (2) non-FillableCell,
  or (3) already-occupied cell.

  Cell family: Cell.fromChar parses map chars to concrete cells; FillableCell holds an optional pipe and renders pipe or
  '.'; TerminationCell holds pointingTo direction and Type (SOURCE/SINK), with filled state for rendering; Wall renders
  wall char.

  TerminationCell.toSingleChar: Returns the corresponding arrow character based on the current termination cell's
  filling state and pointingTo direction: when filled, returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Filled; when unfilled,
  returns the arrow from PipePatterns.Unfilled. Both SOURCE and SINK use pointingTo directly without direction reversal.
  Throws IllegalStateException("Invalid pointingTo value!") for unknown direction.

  Pipe: Expose connection directions via getConnections() returning Direction[] based on Shape; char rendering by
  filled/unfilled state; construct from short string code (HZ, VT, TL, TR, BL, BR, CR). getConnections(): Returns the
  list of connection directions based on the current pipe shape; throws IllegalStateException("Unknown shape") if the
  shape is not recognized. toSingleChar(): Returns the character representation of this pipe, different for filled and
  unfilled pipes.

  Water Flow: fillBeginTile() marks the source cell as filled. fillTiles(distance) fills pipes within the specified
  distance from source using BFS-style expansion. Water propagates along pipes only when adjacent pipes have matching
  connection directions (one pipe's direction must be the opposite of the other). Each round increments the fill
  distance by 1.

  Win/Loss Conditions: checkPath() uses BFS to determine if a connected path exists from SOURCE to SINK by following
  (i) from SOURCE, step once in the SOURCE pointingTo direction, and (ii) from a pipe cell, step in each pipe connection direction.
  It does not require the traversed pipes to be filled; reaching the SINK cell implies win. hasLost() returns true when prevFilledTiles == 0,
  meaning no new pipes were filled in the previous round (loss condition). The game checks loss only after delay has ended (distance > 0).

  PipeQueue: peek/consume/undo head pipe and auto-refill to fixed length with random shapes. undo(Pipe) inserts the pipe
  back to the front of the queue.

  DelayBar: countdown() decrements the current value by 1 each round. distance() returns -currentValue, representing how
  far the water should flow (negative during countdown, positive after delay ends).

  CellStack: push/pop last placement; undoCount counts the number of successful undo actions (i.e., successful pop
  operations), not the current stack size; 

  External/Tool Classes (not generated; handwritten or reused,You only need to assume that these Java files mentioned below are already in the pa19 package.)
  Pa19Helper is a small helper/assembly class used to build core game model objects for this project. It provides
  convenience methods to (1) construct a Game by wiring together Map, PipeQueue, DelayBar, and CellStack from given
  inputs, and (2) create/parse basic elements (e.g., Map from a Cell[][] or string representation, map characters/short
  codes into Cell/Pipe). It also centralizes simple direction utilities (opposite direction and unit offsets) used
  during parsing and movement-related calculations.
  Deserializer: Purpose: deserialize map input into runtime objects. Field: Path path (input file path). Constructors:
  Deserializer(String path) delegates to private Deserializer(Path); private Deserializer(Path) validates file existence
  and throws FileNotFoundException if missing. Methods: Game parseGame() reads rows, cols, delay, map lines, and
  optional default pipes, then returns Pa19Helper.createGame(...); on unexpected EOF or I/O failure, returns null
  (and logs error). static Cell[][] parseString(int rows, int cols, String cellsRep) converts multiline text to a Cell
  matrix by character, using Pa19Helper.cellFromChar with SINK type on border cells and SOURCE type on inner
  cells. private String getFirstNonEmptyLine(BufferedReader br) returns the first non-blank, non-comment line (ignores
  lines starting with #), or null at end of stream.

  PipePatterns: Purpose: central constant repository for all map-rendering characters. Design: final utility class with
  private constructor (non-instantiable). Exposed constants: WALL (solid wall block), plus two nested static groups:
  Filled and Unfilled. Each group defines arrow chars (UP_ARROW, DOWN_ARROW, LEFT_ARROW, RIGHT_ARROW) and pipe-shape
  chars (HORIZONTAL, VERTICAL, TOP_LEFT, TOP_RIGHT, BOTTOM_LEFT, BOTTOM_RIGHT, CROSS). Use PipePatterns constants for
  all rendering chars; do not hardcode glyphs in TerminationCell and PipeShape logic.

  StringUtils: Purpose: provide lightweight string helper utilities. Class design: utility class with a private
  constructor to prevent instantiation. 
```

# Operation Specifications
DelayBar::distance
Summary: Calculates the current water propagation distance based on the delay state.
Algorithm: Return `-currentValue`. During countdown this yields negative values; once countdown ends (value <= 0), it yields positive distances.
Related Features: currentValue
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: int representing the flow distance. Negative means delay active, positive means water propagation range.
Pre-condition: Object is initialized.
Post-condition: No state change.

DelayBar::countdown
Summary: Decrements the active delay timer by one unit per game tick.
Algorithm: `currentValue = currentValue - 1`
Related Features: currentValue
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: `currentValue` is initialized and >= 0.
Post-condition: `currentValue` is reduced by exactly 1.

Post-condition: No state change.

DelayBar::countdown
Summary: Decrements the active delay timer by one unit per game tick.
Algorithm: `currentValue = currentValue - 1`
Related Features: currentValue
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: void
Pre-condition: `currentValue` is initialized and >= 0.
Post-condition: `currentValue` is reduced by exactly 1.
